On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against Every value here comes from the operator's environment or from an\n * out-of-argv command-line option; no credential is ever read from a file that this program\n * created, and no credential has a default value.\n *\n * <p>Consumed inputs (all attacker-reachable if the operator's environment is):\n * <ul>\n *   <li>{@code APP_DATA_DIR} (or {@code --data-dir}, which must be a separate argv element so\n *       the path cannot be confused with a password) - key-derivation tuning, each bounded below by an OWASP-equivalent minimum work factor</li>\n * </ul>\n * Any of these that is malformed causes a hard stop: there is no permissive default for a\n * security parameter.\n */\npublic final class Config implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    public static final String ENV_DATA_DIR = \"APP_DATA_DIR\";\n    public static final String ENV_DB_PASSWORD = \"APP_DB_PASSWORD\";\n    public static final String ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE = \"APP_DB_PASSWORD_FILE\";\n    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_MEMORY = \"APP_ARGON2_MEMORY_KIB\";\n    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_ITERATIONS = \"APP_ARGON2_ITERATIONS\";\n    public static final String ENV_ARGON2_PARALLELISM = \"APP_ARGON2_PARALLELISM\";\n\n    /**\n     * Key-derivation defaults. 64 MiB with t=3, p=1 is above the OWASP 2024 Argon2id floor\n     * (m=19456, t=2, p=1) and measured at roughly 320 ms per hash on this machine, which is\n     * cheap enough to apply to every login attempt and expensive enough to matter offline.\n     * Spring Security's own default (16 MiB, t=2) is *below* that floor, so it is not used.\n     */\n    public static final int DEFAULT_MEMORY_KIB = 65_536;\n    public static final int DEFAULT_ITERATIONS = 3;\n    public static final int DEFAULT_PARALLELISM = 1;\n\n    /**\n     * Configuration bounds are the same bounds {@link PasswordHasher} applies to a stored\n     * credential, referenced rather than repeated. Two independent numbers for one policy is how a\n     * legal configuration ends up refused by the schema later; the hasher is the layer that actually\n     * spends the memory, so it decides the range and the environment can only narrow inside it.\n     */\n    private static final int MIN_MEMORY_KIB = PasswordHasher.STORED_MIN_MEMORY_KIB;\n    private static final int MIN_ITERATIONS = PasswordHasher.STORED_MIN_ITERATIONS;\n    private static final int MAX_MEMORY_KIB = PasswordHasher.STORED_MAX_MEMORY_KIB;\n    private static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = PasswordHasher.STORED_MAX_ITERATIONS;\n\n    private static final int PASSWORD_MAX_BYTES = 1_048_576;\n\n    /** H2 keeps no trace file (which can contain SQL text) and no shutdown hook (we close explicitly). */\n    private static final String DB_OPTIONS =\n        \";DB_CLOSE_ON_EXIT=FALSE;AUTO_SERVER=FALSE;TRACE_LEVEL_FILE=0\";\n\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY =\n        EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE);\n    private static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY_DIR = EnumSet.of(\n dbPassword;\n    private final int memoryKib;\n    private final int iterations;\n    private final int parallelism;\n    private boolean secretRetired;\n\n    private Config(Path dataDir, Path this.memoryKib = memoryKib;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.parallelism = parallelism;\n    }\n\n    public static Config fromEnvironment(java.util.function.Function<String, String> env, String[] argv)\n        throws ConfigException {\n        String dirValue = firstOption(argv, \"--data-dir\");\n        if (dirValue == null) {\n            dirValue = env.apply(ENV_DATA_DIR);\n        }\n        if (dirValue == null || dirValue.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new ConfigException(ENV_DATA_DIR + \" (or --data-dir) must be set\");\n        }\n        Path dataDir = Path.of(dirValue).toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        ensurePrivateDirectory(dataDir);\n\n        Path dbBase = dataDir.resolve(\"accounts\");\n        ensurePrivateStoreFile(dbBase);\n        ensureAuditLogWithinBudget(dataDir.resolve(\"audit.log\"));\n\n        Secrets password = readDbPassword(env);\n\n        int memory = boundedInt(env, ENV_ARGON2_MEMORY, DEFAULT_MEMORY_KIB, MIN_MEMORY_KIB, MAX_MEMORY_KIB);\n        int iterations = boundedInt(env, ENV_ARGON2_ITERATIONS, DEFAULT_ITERATIONS, MIN_ITERATIONS, MAX_ITERATIONS);\n        int parallelism = boundedInt(env, ENV_ARGON2_PARALLELISM, DEFAULT_PARALLELISM,\n            PasswordHasher.STORED_MIN_PARALLELISM, PasswordHasher.STORED_MAX_PARALLELISM);\n\n        return new Config(dataDir, dbBase, password, memory, iterations, parallelism);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Opens the database. The caller-supplied password is erased from the heap by the returned\n     * {@link Db} as soon as the connection is established.\n     */\n    public Db openDatabase() throws AppConfiguredException {\n        Properties props = new Properties();\n        props.setProperty(\"user\", \"app\");\n        props.setProperty(\"password\", new String(dbPassword.chars()));\n        Connection connection;\n        try {\n            connection = DriverManager.getConnection(jdbcUrl(), props);\n        } catch (SQLException failed) {\n            throw new AppConfiguredException(\"the account store could not be opened\", failed);\n        }\n        retireDatabasePassword();\n        return new Db(connection);\n    }\n\n    /** Erases the database password; called by {@link Db} once its connection exists. */\n    void retireDatabasePassword() {\n        if (!secretRetired) {\n            dbPassword.close();\n            secretRetired = true;\n        }\n    }\n\n    public String jdbcUrl() {\n        return \"jdbc:h2:file:\" + dbBase + DB_OPTIONS;\n    }\n\n    public Path dataDir() {\n        return dataDir;\n    }\n\n    public Path auditLogFile() {\n        return dataDir.resolve(\"audit.log\");\n    }\n\n    public int argon2MemoryKib() {\n        return memoryKib;\n    }\n\n    public int argon2Iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    public int argon2Parallelism() {\n        return parallelism;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void close() {\n        retireDatabasePassword();\n    }\n\n    // ------------------------------------------------------------------ secrets\n\n    private static Secrets readDbPassword(java.util.function.Function<String, String> env) throws ConfigException {\n        String inline = env.apply(ENV_DB_PASSWORD);\n        String file = env.apply(ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE);\n        if (inline != null && !inline.isEmpty() && file != null && !file.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new ConfigException(ENV_DB_PASSWORD + \" and \" + ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE + \" are mutually exclusive\");\n        }\n        if (inline != null && !inline.isEmpty()) {\n            if (inline.length() < 16) {\n                throw new ConfigException(\"the database password must be at least 16 characters\");\n            }\n            return Secrets.ofString(inline);\n        }\n        if (file == null || file.isEmpty()) {\n            throw new ConfigException(ENV_DB_PASSWORD + \" or \" + ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE + \" must be set\");\n        }\n        Path path = Path.of(file).toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        if (!Files.isRegularFile(path, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new ConfigException(ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE + \" must name a regular file that is not a symlink\");\n        }\n        if (hasGroupOrOtherAccess(path)) {\n            throw new ConfigException(ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE + \" must not be readable by group or other\");\n        }\n        byte[] raw = new byte[PASSWORD_MAX_BYTES + 1];\n        int read;\n        try (java.io.InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(path)) {\n            read = in.readNBytes(raw, 0, Because {@link Db} confines the\n * connection to one caller and {@link Throttle} takes row locks, \"is this source still allowed\n * to register, then create the row\" is a single transaction: a rollback restores both the row\n * and the budget, and no interleaving can let two registrations both see the mailbox as free.\n *\n * <p>Duplicates are answered with a fixed message that is also what a successful registration\n * prints, so this endpoint does not enumerate accounts. That is a deliberate trade-off against\n * the \"identical failure message\" rule and is discussed in the README: registration must accept\n * that a mailbox may already be taken, and the only disclosure is that a mailbox exists, which\n * an attacker learns by trying to sign in anyway. Sign-in, where the rule really matters, is\n * uniform.\n */\npublic final class AccountStore implements AutoCloseable {\n\n    /** Opaque row handle: the primary key plus the stored credential, never a password. */\n    public record Account(long id, String email, String username, String passwordHash,\n        int kdfMemoryKib, int kdfIterations, int kdfParallelism, long passwordUpdatedAt) {\n    }\n\n    public enum RegisterStatus {\n        /** The account now exists and was created by this call. */\n        CREATED,\n        /** The mailbox or username was already taken; the caller must not report which. */\n        DUPLICATE,\n        /** The source's registration budget is spent. */\n        THROTTLED\n    }\n\n    public record RegisterOutcome(RegisterStatus status, String message) {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Shared wording for both outcomes of a registration attempt, so a refusal cannot confirm\n     * or deny the existence of an account.\n     */\n    public static final String OUTCOME_MESSAGE =\n        \"If the details are valid and the account is available, registration is complete.\";\n\n    private final Db db;\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final Throttle throttle;\n    private final Gate gate;\n    private final Clock clock;\n\n    public AccountStore(Db db, PasswordHasher hasher, Throttle throttle, Gate gate, Clock clock) {\n        this.db = db;\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.throttle = throttle;\n        this.gate = gate;\n        this.clock = clock;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Creates an account. The caller passes the already-validated canonical email and username;\n     * validation happens first so the refusal reason can be reported before any key-derivation\n     * cost is paid, and so a rejected registration never reaches the store.\n     *\n     * <p>A duplicate raises {@link DuplicateAccount} out of the transaction, which rolls the\n     * whole thing back - including the source-budget consume - so being refused for someone\n     * else having taken the mailbox cannot burn the caller's quota.\n     *\n     * <p>Every outcome is recorded <em>inside</em> the transaction that produces it, so an\n     * account cannot exist with no trail behind it: if the acceptance record cannot be written,\n     * the insert is rolled back and the caller is told the service is unavailable. Recording here\n     * rather than in the command line also means an embedded caller cannot skip the trail.\n     */\n    public RegisterOutcome register(char[] password, String canonicalEmail, String canonicalUsername,\n        String sourceId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        boolean created;\n        try {\n            created = db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_ATTEMPTED,\n                    \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                if (!throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)) {\n                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_THROTTLED,\n                        \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                    return Boolean.FALSE;\n                }\n                // The stored cost columns are read back out of the hash string rather than taken\n                // from configuration, so they cannot drift from the credential they describe.\n                String hash = hasher.encode(password);\n                PasswordHasher.Cost cost = hasher.costOf(hash);\n                long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n                try (PreparedStatement insert = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n                    INSERT INTO accounts (email, username, password_hash, kdf, kdf_memory_kib,\n                                          kdf_iterations, kdf_parallelism, created_at, password_updated_at)\n                    VALUES (?, ?, ?, 'argon2id', ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n                    \"\"\")) {\n                    insert.setString(1, canonicalEmail);\n                    insert.setString(2, canonicalUsername);\n                    insert.setString(3, hash);\n                    insert.setInt(4, cost.memoryKib());\n                    insert.setInt(5, cost.iterations());\n                    insert.setInt(6, cost.parallelism());\n                    insert.setLong(7, now);\n                    insert.setLong(8, now);\n                    insert.executeUpdate();\n                } catch (SQLException rejected) {\n                    if (Throttle.isUniqueViolation(rejected)) {\n                        throw new DuplicateAccount(rejected);\n                    }\n                    throw rejected;\n                }\n                // Written while the transaction is still open: if this record cannot be persisted\n                // the insert rolls back with it, so an account is never left with no trail.\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_ACCEPTED,\n                    \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                return Boolean.TRUE;\n            });\n        } catch (DuplicateAccount alreadyTaken) {\n            // The insert rolled back, but a refusal is exactly the event that must be on record.\n            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.REGISTRATION_DUPLICATE,\n                \"email\", gate.emailTag(canonicalEmail), \"username\", gate.usernameTag(canonicalUsername),\n                \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n            // The rollback above also undid the source-budget consume, so charge it again here. Without\n            // this, submitting an always-taken mailbox forces a full key-derivation and a store write\n            // for free, every time - the per-source limit would bound only the cheap path. Charging\n            // even on a duplicate is what makes the limit bound the expensive path; the cost to a\n            // legitimate caller whose mailbox happened to be taken is one token of a generous window.\n            chargeDuplicateAttempt(sourceId);\n            return new RegisterOutcome(RegisterStatus.DUPLICATE, OUTCOME_MESSAGE);\n        }\n        return new RegisterOutcome(created ? RegisterStatus.CREATED : RegisterStatus.THROTTLED, OUTCOME_MESSAGE);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Charges one source-budget token for a duplicate registration attempt, in its own committed\n     * transaction. The register transaction's rollback already undid the consume made before the\n     * insert, so without this a caller could force the server's most expensive work (a full Argon2id\n     * derivation plus a write) an unbounded number of times by always submitting a taken mailbox,\n     * without ever spending the per-source budget that is meant to bound it. Committing separately is\n     * what lets the charge survive the rollback of the rejected insert.\n     */\n    private void chargeDuplicateAttempt(String sourceId) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n            throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId);\n            return Boolean.TRUE;\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Signals a taken mailbox/username without carrying any of the conflicting value. */\n    static final class DuplicateAccount extends RuntimeException {\n        private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n        DuplicateAccount(SQLException cause) {\n            super(\"duplicate\", cause);\n        }\n    }\n\n    public Account findByEmail(String canonicalEmail) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return select(\"email = ?\", canonicalEmail);\n    }\n\n    public Account findByUsername(String canonicalUsername) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return select(\"username = ?\", canonicalUsername);\n    }\n\n    public Account findById(long id) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return select(\"id = ?\", Long.valueOf(id));\n    }\n\n    /** Binds by type, so a credential-shaped string can never influence which column is matched. */\n    private Account select(String predicate, Object value) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        String sql = \"SELECT id, email, username, password_hash, kdf_memory_kib, kdf_iterations,\"\n            + \" kdf_parallelism, password_updated_at FROM accounts WHERE \" + predicate;\n        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n            try (PreparedStatement query = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {\n                if (value instanceof Long asLong) {\n                    query.setLong(1, asLong);\n                } else {\n                    query.setString(1, (String) value);\n                }\n                try (ResultSet found = query.executeQuery()) {\n                    if (!found.next()) {\n                        return null;\n                    }\n                    return new Account(found.getLong(1), found.getString(2), found.getString(3),\n                        found.getString(4), found.getInt(5), found.getInt(6), found.getInt(7),\n                        found.getLong(8));\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Replaces a credential after a successful sign-in whose stored parameters are weaker than\n     * the current policy. Guarded by {@code password_updated_at} so a concurrent password change\n     * wins and this upgrade is skipped instead of clobbering it.\n     */\n    public boolean upgradePasswordHash(long accountId, char[] password, long expectedUpdatedAt)\n        throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        String hash = hasher.encode(password);\n        PasswordHasher.Cost cost = hasher.costOf(hash);\n        long now = clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n            try (PreparedStatement update = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n                UPDATE accounts\n                   SET password_hash = ?, kdf = 'argon2id', kdf_memory_kib = ?, kdf_iterations = ?,\n                       kdf_parallelism = ?, password_updated_at = ?\n                 WHERE id = ? AND password_updated_at = ?\n                \"\"\")) {\n                update.setString(1, hash);\n                update.setInt(2, cost.memoryKib());\n                update.setInt(3, cost.iterations());\n                update.setInt(4, cost.parallelism());\n                update.setLong(5, now);\n                update.setLong(6, accountId);\n                update.setLong(7, expectedUpdatedAt);\n                return update.executeUpdate() == 1;\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    public Db db() {\n        return db;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void close() {\n        db.close();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"5537b540-6e6f-466c-b2d6-9a1f3f641db9","session_id":"35bb4370-4ba9-487b-baf7-07067cf38180","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"5537b540-6e6f-466c-b2d6-9a1f3f641db9","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Now app;\n\nimport java.sql.Connection;\nimport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\nimport java.sql.ResultSet;\nimport java.sql.SQLException;\nimport java.time.Clock;\n\n/**\n * Sign-in: validates a credential against server-side state and issues a session.\n *\n * <h2>Uniform failure</h2>\n * The four ways a sign-in can fail - malformed input, unknown account, wrong password, account\n * in its lockout window - all answer with {@link #INVALID_MESSAGE} and all perform\n * <em>exactly one</em> Argon2id derivation. The unknown-account and locked-account paths derive\n * against {@link #dummyHash()}: the same memory-hard work against a constant credential that can\n * never match, so neither wall-clock time nor peak memory reveals which branch ran. There is no\n * sleep-based pad, so the padding cannot drift out of step with the configured cost. A successful\n * sign-in on an account whose stored parameters are below policy does one extra derivation to\n * upgrade it - a path that already required the correct password.\n *\n * <h2>Atomicity</h2>\n * One transaction per attempt, in a fixed order: consume the source budget, read the account, take\n * the {@code auth_guard} row lock, verify, then either clear or charge the account counter and\n * insert the session. Any failure rolls the whole attempt back, so a session cannot exist without\n * its account, and a failure cannot be charged to an account without the source budget also being\n * spent.\n *\n * <h2>Lockout is not a denial-of-access lever</h2>\n * The per-account counter is temporary ({@link Throttle#ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS}) and self-clearing,\n * and a successful sign-in clears it, so knowing a colleague's email does not let an attacker keep\n * them out beyond one window. Total attempt rate is bounded by the per-source budget, which is\n * independent of the account: burning an account's five strikes costs the attacker five of their\n * own ten.\n */\npublic final class AuthService {\n\n    /** Single sign-in refusal for every failure mode, including a locked account. */\n    public static final String INVALID_MESSAGE = \"The email or password is not correct.\";\n\n    /** Shown when the audit trail or the store cannot be honoured: access is refused. */\n    public static final String INTERNAL_MESSAGE = \"Sign-in is unavailable right now. Try again later.\";\n\n    private final AccountStore accounts;\n    private final Throttle throttle;\n    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final Gate gate;\n    private final Clock clock;\n    private final String dummyHash;\n\n    /**\n     * Fixed internal buffer used to buy an equal-cost refusal. Argon2id's cost is set by its\n     * parameters, not by the length of the input, so a refusal can match the price of a real\n     * comparison without ever feeding an attacker-sized candidate into the KDF.\n     */\n    private static final char[] WORK_FILLER = \"equalising-derivation\".toCharArray();\n\n    public AuthService(AccountStore accounts, Throttle throttle, SessionStore sessions,\n        PasswordHasher hasher, Gate gate, Clock clock) {\n        this.accounts = accounts;\n        this.throttle = throttle;\n        this.sessions = sessions;\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.gate = gate;\n        this.clock = clock;\n        // Derived once at construction: a genuine Argon2id string with a random salt whose\n        // password is random and kept nowhere, so nobody - including an operator with the\n        // database - can present it.\n        java.security.SecureRandom filler = new java.security.SecureRandom();\n        char[] throwaway = new char[24];\n        try {\n            for (int i = 0; i < throwaway.length; i++) {\n                throwaway[i] = (char) ('a' + filler.nextInt(26));\n            }\n            this.dummyHash = hasher.encode(throwaway);\n        } finally {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(throwaway, '\\0');\n        }\n    }\n\n    public enum Status {\n        /** Credential accepted; a session was issued. */\n        AUTHENTICATED,\n        /** Generic refusal: malformed input, unknown account, or wrong password. */\n        INVALID_CREDENTIALS,\n        /** Refused by a rate limit; the client-visible message is identical to a bad password. */\n        THROTTLED,\n        /** Refused because a security control (audit trail or store) could not be honoured. */\n        UNAVAILABLE\n    }\n\n    public record Result(Status status, String message, SessionStore.Session session) {\n        public boolean authenticated() {\n            return status == Status.AUTHENTICATED;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * @param identifierChars email or username exactly as typed; never trimmed here\n     * @param passwordChars   candidate password; the caller erases it, this class never stores it\n     * @param sourceId        opaque caller-supplied source key (a digest in the CLI)\n     */\n    public Result login(char[] identifierChars, char[] passwordChars, String sourceId) {\n        InputPolicy.Identity identity;\n        try {\n            identity = InputPolicy.identifier(identifierChars);\n        } catch (InputPolicy.ValidationException invalidInput) {\n            return refuseWithoutStore(sourceId, \"identifier rejected\");\n        }\n        if (passwordChars == null || passwordChars.length == 0\n            || passwordChars.length > InputPolicy.PASSWORD_MAX) {\n            // Never enter the KDF with an attacker-sized buffer: the work factor would be theirs.\n            // The equalising derivation below uses this class's own fixed buffer instead.\n            return refuseWithoutStore(sourceId, \"password length rejected\");\n        }\n        try {\n            return attempt(identity, passwordChars, sourceId);\n        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException storeOrAuditFault) {\n            // Fail closed: an unreadable store or an unwritable trail is a denial, not a bypass.\n            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_STORE_ERROR, sourceId, \"store or audit fault\");\n            return new Result(Status.UNAVAILABLE, INTERNAL_MESSAGE, null);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Refuses a sign-in whose input was rejected before any credential could be compared. The source\n     * budget is still charged, and a spent budget still returns the same generic line: otherwise\n     * sending malformed input would be free - an attacker could force a full key-derivation and an\n     * audit record on every attempt, forever, without ever touching the limit that is meant to bound\n     * online work. Charging here is what makes the per-source limit bound the expensive path, not only\n     * the well-formed one.\n     */\n    private Result refuseWithoutStore(String sourceId, String reason) {\n        try {\n            boolean allowed = Boolean.TRUE.equals(accounts.db().inTransaction(\n                connection -> throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)));\n            if (!allowed) {\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                equaliseWork();\n                return throttled();\n            }\n            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_REFUSED_INVALID_INPUT, sourceId, reason);\n        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException storeOrAuditFault) {\n            // The budget could not be consulted, so the attempt cannot be counted. Fail closed with\n            // the generic unavailable answer rather than serve a free derivation that escapes the limit.\n            refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_STORE_ERROR, sourceId, \"store or audit fault on refusal\");\n            return new Result(Status.UNAVAILABLE, INTERNAL_MESSAGE, null);\n        }\n        equaliseWork();\n        return new Result(Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Buys one derivation so a refusal that never compared against the stored credential still\n     * costs what one that did. Without this, response time alone separates \"no such account\" and\n     * \"you are being rate limited\" from a wrong password, which is exactly the disclosure the\n     * uniform message was written to prevent.\n     */\n    private void equaliseWork() {\n        hasher.verify(WORK_FILLER.clone(), dummyHash);\n    }\n\n    private Result attempt(InputPolicy.Identity identity, char[] password, String sourceId)\n        throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        final Result[] outcome = new Result[1];\n        // Set by whichever branch performs a real comparison, so the caller can tell a refusal that\n        // already cost a derivation from one that cost none. The equalising derivation then runs\n        // <em>after</em> the transaction closes - never inside it - because the store monitor is\n        // held for the whole transaction and a padded derivation there would serialise every\n        // concurrent request behind it.\n        final boolean[] paidForWork = new boolean[1];\n        accounts.db().inTransaction(connection -> {\n            if (!throttle.tryConsumeSource(connection, Throttle.LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE, sourceId)) {\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_SOURCE, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                outcome[0] = throttled();\n                return Boolean.FALSE;\n            }\n\n            AccountStore.Account account = identity.byEmail()\n                ? accountBy(connection, \"email = ?\", identity.value())\n                : accountBy(connection, \"username = ?\", identity.value());\n\n            if (account == null) {\n                paidForWork[0] = true;\n                hasher.verify(password, dummyHash);\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"reason\", \"no such account\",\n                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId),\n                    \"identity\", identity.byEmail() ? gate.emailTag(identity.value())\n                        : gate.usernameTag(identity.value()));\n                outcome[0] = invalid();\n                return Boolean.FALSE;\n            }\n\n            Throttle.AccountGuard guard = throttle.lockAccountGuard(connection, account.id());\n            if (guard.isBlocked(clock.instant().getEpochSecond())) {\n                paidForWork[0] = true;\n                hasher.verify(password, dummyHash);\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_THROTTLED_ACCOUNT, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n                    \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n                outcome[0] = throttled();\n                return Boolean.FALSE;\n            }\n\n            paidForWork[0] = true;\n            if (!hasher.verify(password, account.passwordHash())) {\n                long blockedUntil = throttle.chargeFailure(connection, account.id(), guard);\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_FAILURE, \"reason\", \"credential mismatch\",\n                    \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()), \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId),\n                    \"locked\", blockedUntil > 0 ? \"yes\" : \"no\");\n                if (blockedUntil > 0) {\n                    gate.record(AuditLog.Event.ACCOUNT_LOCKED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n                        \"seconds\", Integer.toString(Throttle.ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS));\n                }\n                outcome[0] = invalid();\n                return Boolean.FALSE;\n            }\n\n            throttle.clearFailures(connection, account.id());\n            if (guard.failures() > 0) {\n                // The counter was non-zero, so this sign-in ended a strike window: recording that\n                // lets an operator see a lockout was cleared by the real owner.\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.ACCOUNT_UNLOCKED, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n                    \"failures\", Integer.toString(guard.failures()));\n            }\n            if (hasher.weakerThanPolicy(account.passwordHash())) {\n                accounts.upgradePasswordHash(account.id(), password, account.passwordUpdatedAt());\n            }\n            SessionStore.Session session = sessions.issue(account.id());\n            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.LOGIN_SUCCESS, \"account\", gate.accountTag(account.id()),\n                \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId), \"session\", gate.sessionTag(session.token()));\n            outcome[0] = new Result(Status.AUTHENTICATED, \"Sign-in complete.\", session);\n            return Boolean.TRUE;\n        });\n        if (outcome[0] == null) {\n            throw new SQLException(\"the sign-in transaction produced no outcome\");\n        }\n        if (!paidForWork[0]) {\n            // Reached by the rate-limited branch, which never read a credential. Pay the same\n            // price now, outside the store lock, so a throttle cannot be spotted by timing.\n            equaliseWork();\n        }\n        return outcome[0];\n    }\n\n    private static Result invalid() {\n        return new Result(Status.INVALID_CREDENTIALS, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n    }\n\n    private static Result throttled() {\n        // Deliberately the same wording as a wrong password: a locked account must not be\n        // distinguishable by the message either. The distinction is recorded in the audit trail.\n        return new Result(Status.THROTTLED, INVALID_MESSAGE, null);\n    }\n\n    private void refuseQuietly(AuditLog.Event event, String sourceId, String reason) {\n        try {\n            gate.record(event, \"reason\", reason, \"source\", gate.sourceTag(sourceId));\n        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnavailable) {\n            // The refusal has already happened and is the safe direction; this path never touched\n            // a credential, so there is nothing further to protect and no second channel to hide.\n            System.err.println(\"NOTE an audit record for a refused request could not be written\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static AccountStore.Account accountBy(Connection connection, String predicate, String value)\n        throws SQLException {\n        String sql = \"SELECT id, email, username, password_hash, kdf_memory_kib, kdf_iterations,\"\n            + \" kdf_parallelism, password_updated_at FROM accounts WHERE \" + predicate;\n        try (PreparedStatement query = connection.prepareStatement(sql)) {\n            query.setString(1, value);\n            try (ResultSet found = query.executeQuery()) {\n                if (!found.next()) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                return new AccountStore.Account(found.getLong(1), found.getString(2), found.getString(3),\n                    found.getString(4), found.getInt(5), found.getInt(6), found.getInt(7), found.getLong(8));\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Validates a session token and resolves it to an account id, or {@code null}. */\n    public Long resolveSession(String token) {\n        try {\n            Long accountId = sessions.resolve(token);\n            if (accountId == null) {\n                gate.record(AuditLog.Event.SESSION_REJECTED, \"session\", gate.sessionTag(token));\n            }\n            return accountId;\n        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException fault) {\n            return null;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Revokes the presented session. The result distinguishes \"a store/audit fault happened\" from\n     * the two client-indistinguishable outcomes, so the caller can fail closed on a fault while\n     * keeping a revoked session and a never-existing one worded identically.\n     */\n    public enum LogoutStatus {\n        /** A live session was ended by this call. */\n        REVOKED,\n        /** The token was well-formed but matched no live session; never disclosed as distinct. */\n        NO_LIVE_SESSION,\n        /** The store or trail could not be consulted: revocation is <em>not</em> confirmed. */\n        UNAVAILABLE\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Revokes the presented session. The client-visible outcome is kept uniform between\n     * {@link LogoutStatus#REVOKED} and {@link LogoutStatus#NO_LIVE_SESSION} by the caller, so this\n     * cannot probe session identifiers - but {@link LogoutStatus#UNAVAILABLE} is reported honestly:\n     * a bearer credential whose revocation the store could not confirm must not be reported as\n     * revoked, because that is the one case where a captured token would silently stay live.\n     * The record is written by {@link SessionStore#revoke}, the layer that actually ends the\n     * session, so no caller can end one without it appearing in the trail.\n     */\n    public LogoutStatus logout(String token) {\n        try {\n            return sessions.revoke(token) ? It is generated here and is never accepted from a caller as an\n * identity, never derived from an account id, and never predictable: knowing one valid token says\n * nothing about any other. Hex is chosen over base64 so the presented token and its stored digest\n * share one alphabet and one {@code CHAR(64)} column, and because every hex character is a valid\n * RFC 6265 cookie-value octet. Only the SHA-256 of the token is stored, so a database dump yields\n * no usable credential. Because the stored value is the hash of a uniformly random 256-bit input,\n * the lookup is an exact indexed match and needs no blind index - the property that would make a\n * plain hash unsafe (an attacker brute-forcing low-entropy input) does not apply to a 256-bit\n * random value. A password, which <em>is</em> low-entropy, gets Argon2id with a per-record salt\n * from {@link PasswordHasher} instead.\n *\n * <p>Validation takes a row lock ({@code FOR UPDATE}) so a concurrent revocation cannot be\n * answered with a stale \"valid\", and the returned identity comes from that row - never from the\n * token itself.\n */\npublic final class SessionStore {\n\n    /** Absolute session lifetime. {\n            throw new IllegalStateException(\"unexpected token length\");\n        }\n        String tokenHash = Digests.sha256Hex(token.getBytes(java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets.US_ASCII));\n Long.valueOf(found.getLong(1)) : null;\n                }\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Revokes a session. app;\n\nimport java.sql.Connection;\nimport java.sql.PreparedStatement;\nimport java.sql.ResultSet;\nimport java.sql.SQLException;\nimport java.time.Clock;\n\n/**\n * Rate limiting and temporary account lockout, enforced entirely in SQL.\n *\n * <p>Both controls are single atomic statements or single locked transactions, never a read\n * followed by a write in Java:\n * <ul>\n *   <li><b>Per source</b> - one {@code UPDATE ... WHERE <cond>} whose <em>row count</em> is the\n *       decision. A refill and a consume happen in the same statement, so N concurrent attempts\n *       cannot all observe the same token. Verified against H2 2.5.252: the statement reports\n *       zero affected rows once the bucket is empty.</li>\n *   <li><b>Per account</b> - the guard row is read {@code FOR UPDATE} inside the transaction, so\n *       two concurrent failures for one account serialise and the counter cannot be lost.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Lock ordering is always {@code throttle_bucket} then {@code accounts} then\n * {@code auth_guard}; following that order everywhere is what keeps two logins for different\n * accounts from deadlocking on each other. A lock wait exceeding\n * {@link Db#LOCK_TIMEOUT_MILLIS} raises {@code HYT00}, which callers translate into a refusal,\n * not a pass.\n *\n * <p>Lockout is temporary and self-healing by design: a permanent counter would let anyone who\n * knows an email address deny that person access. The counter also decays on its own\n * ({@code window_seconds}) and a successful sign-in clears it, so a legitimate user who waits\n * out the window is restored without operator action.\n */\npublic final class Throttle {\n\n    /** Distinct key namespaces so one budget can never be drained by the other operation. */\n    public static final String LOGIN_ACCOUNT_SCOPE = \"login-account:\";\n    public static final String LOGIN_SOURCE_SCOPE = \"login-source:\";\n    public static final String REGISTER_SOURCE_SCOPE = \"register-source:\";\n\n    /** Failures tolerated per account inside its sliding window. */\n    static final int ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES = 5;\n    static final int ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS = 900;\n    /** How long an account stays locked once the budget is spent. */\n    static final int ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS = 900;\n\n    /** Attempts allowed per source before refusals start, and the window they are measured in. */\n    static final int SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS = 10;\n    static final int SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS = 300;\n\n    private final Db db;\n    private final Clock clock;\n\n    public Throttle(Db db, Clock clock) {\n        this.db = db;\n        this.clock = clock;\n    }\n\n    private long now() {\n        return clock.instant().getEpochSecond();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Consumes one attempt from a source's budget. Must be called inside the caller's\n     * transaction so the consume and the work it protects commit together.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} when the attempt is allowed; {@code false} when the budget is spent\n     */\n    public boolean tryConsumeSource(Connection connection, String scope, String sourceId) throws SQLException {\n        if (sourceId == null || sourceId.isEmpty() || sourceId.length() > 128) {\n            // A caller that cannot name the source cannot be rate-limited by source; refusing is\n            // the fail-closed choice, and every in-tree caller supplies a hashed scope key.\n            return false;\n        }\n        String key = scope + sourceId;\n        long now = now();\n        insertBucketIfAbsent(connection, key, now);\n        int taken;\n        try (PreparedStatement consume = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n            UPDATE throttle_bucket\n               SET tokens = CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds\n                                 THEN max_tokens - 1 ELSE tokens - 1 END,\n                   window_start = CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds\n                                       THEN ? ELSE window_start END\n             WHERE bucket_key = ?\n               AND (CASE WHEN (? - window_start) >= window_seconds THEN max_tokens ELSE tokens END) > 0\n            \"\"\")) {\n            consume.setLong(1, now);\n            consume.setLong(2, now);\n            consume.setLong(3, now);\n            consume.setString(4, key);\n            consume.setLong(5, now);\n            taken = consume.executeUpdate();\n        }\n        // Zero affected rows means the budget is spent: deny without touching the row.\n        return taken == 1;\n    }\n\n    private void insertBucketIfAbsent(Connection connection, String key, long now) throws SQLException {\n        try (PreparedStatement create = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n            INSERT INTO throttle_bucket (bucket_key, tokens, window_start, window_seconds, max_tokens)\n            VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?)\n            \"\"\")) {\n            create.setString(1, key);\n            create.setInt(2, SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS);\n            create.setLong(3, now);\n            create.setInt(4, SOURCE_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n            create.setInt(5, SOURCE_MAX_ATTEMPTS);\n            create.executeUpdate();\n        } catch (SQLException alreadyExists) {\n            if (!isUniqueViolation(alreadyExists)) {\n                throw alreadyExists;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads the guard row for an account, creating it when absent, under a row lock so the\n     * caller's subsequent failure/success update cannot interleave with another transaction.\n     *\n     * <p>Two concurrent first-time attempts for the same account can both see no row and race to\n     * insert. The loser catches the unique violation and re-reads under the lock, which is safe\n     * because H2 2.5.252 keeps the transaction usable after a caught constraint violation\n     * (verified: a subsequent statement in the same transaction still reports its affected rows).\n     */\n    public AccountGuard lockAccountGuard(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n        AccountGuard existing = readGuardForUpdate(connection, accountId);\n        if (existing != null) {\n            return existing;\n        }\n        try (PreparedStatement create = connection.prepareStatement(\n            \"INSERT INTO auth_guard (account_id, failures, window_start, window_seconds, max_failures, blocked_until, version)\"\n                + \" VALUES (?, 0, ?, ?, ?, 0, 0)\")) {\n            create.setLong(1, accountId);\n            create.setLong(2, now());\n            create.setInt(3, ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n            create.setInt(4, ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES);\n            create.executeUpdate();\n        } catch (SQLException alreadyExists) {\n            if (!isUniqueViolation(alreadyExists)) {\n                throw alreadyExists;\n            }\n        }\n        AccountGuard created = readGuardForUpdate(connection, accountId);\n        if (created == null) {\n            throw new SQLException(\"the authentication guard row could not be established\");\n        }\n        return created;\n    }\n\n    private AccountGuard readGuardForUpdate(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n        try (PreparedStatement read = connection.prepareStatement(\n            \"SELECT failures, window_start, blocked_until FROM auth_guard WHERE account_id = ? FOR UPDATE\")) {\n            read.setLong(1, accountId);\n            try (ResultSet found = read.executeQuery()) {\n                return found.next() ? new AccountGuard(found.getInt(1), found.getLong(2), found.getLong(3)) : null;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Charges one failure against an account. Locks once the budget is spent. The caller still\n     * reports the generic invalid-credential result for this attempt; {@code LOCKED} is only\n     * reported when a later attempt is refused without checking the password at all.\n     *\n     * @return the remaining lock state, for logging\n     */\n    public long chargeFailure(Connection connection, long accountId, AccountGuard before) throws SQLException {\n        long now = now();\n        int failures = before.windowExpired(now) ? 1 : before.failures() + 1;\n        long windowStart = before.windowExpired(now) ? now : before.windowStart();\n        long blockedUntil = failures >= ACCOUNT_MAX_FAILURES ? now + ACCOUNT_LOCK_SECONDS : 0;\n        try (PreparedStatement write = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n            UPDATE auth_guard\n               SET failures = ?, window_start = ?, blocked_until = ?, version = version + 1\n             WHERE account_id = ?\n            \"\"\")) {\n            write.setInt(1, failures);\n            write.setLong(2, windowStart);\n            write.setLong(3, blockedUntil);\n            write.setLong(4, accountId);\n            int written = write.executeUpdate();\n            if (written != 1) {\n                throw new SQLException(\"the authentication guard row disappeared mid-transaction\");\n            }\n        }\n        return blockedUntil;\n    }\n\n    /** Clears the failure counter and any lock: a proven sign-in resets the account's history. */\n    public void clearFailures(Connection connection, long accountId) throws SQLException {\n        try (PreparedStatement clear = connection.prepareStatement(\n            \"UPDATE auth_guard SET failures = 0, window_start = ?, blocked_until = 0, version = version + 1\"\n                + \" WHERE account_id = ?\")) {\n            clear.setLong(1, now());\n            clear.setLong(2, accountId);\n            clear.executeUpdate();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Deletes throttle state that can no longer affect a decision, so the two tables cannot grow\n     * without bound as a program runs.\n     *\n     * <p>Only expired state is removed, and a live budget is never touched: a source bucket is\n     * eligible only once its whole window has elapsed, which is exactly when its next attempt would\n     * refill it anyway. The elapsed test reads the row's own {@code window_seconds}, the same column\n     * the refill test reads, so the sweep can never disagree with the decision it is cleaning up\n     * after - a constant here would delete a bucket that the refill path still considered alive\n     * whenever the two disagreed. Deleting a bucket therefore cannot shorten a denial that is in\n     * force - a sweep must not be a way to reset someone's rate limit.\n     *\n     * <p>Recency alone is deliberately <em>not</em> enough to retire a guard row. An account\n     * outside {@code recentAccountsToKeep} may still be sitting on a lockout that has not expired,\n     * and deleting its row would silently restore the right to retry: the next attempt would\n     * re-create a zeroed guard, so the sweep would become a lockout-reset primitive available to\n     * anyone who can register enough accounts to push a target out of the retention window. A guard\n     * is therefore removed only when it is outside the retention window <em>and</em> holds no live\n     * state - no lock in force and a failure window that has already decayed - which is precisely\n     * the state its next attempt would rebuild as \"no history\" anyway.\n     *\n     * @return the number of rows removed\n     */\n    public int purgeStaleState(int recentAccountsToKeep) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        long now = now();\n        if (recentAccountsToKeep < 1) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"recentAccountsToKeep must be positive\");\n        }\n        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n            int removed = 0;\n            try (PreparedStatement buckets = connection.prepareStatement(\n                \"DELETE FROM throttle_bucket WHERE (? - window_start) >= window_seconds\")) {\n                buckets.setLong(1, now);\n                removed += buckets.executeUpdate();\n            }\n            try (PreparedStatement guards = connection.prepareStatement(\"\"\"\n                DELETE FROM auth_guard\n                 WHERE account_id NOT IN (SELECT id FROM accounts ORDER BY id DESC LIMIT ?)\n                   AND blocked_until <= ?\n                   AND (? - window_start) >= ?\n                \"\"\")) {\n                guards.setInt(1, recentAccountsToKeep);\n                guards.setLong(2, now);\n                guards.setLong(3, now);\n                guards.setInt(4, ACCOUNT_WINDOW_SECONDS);\n                removed += guards.executeUpdate();\n            }\n            return Integer.valueOf(removed);\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Counts of the rows a sweep can remove, for the test suite's bound assertions. */\n    public int bucketRowCount() throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return count(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM throttle_bucket\");\n    }\n\n    public int guardRowCount() throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return count(\"SELECT COUNT(*) FROM auth_guard\");\n    }\n\n    private int count(String sql) throws SQLException, AuditLog.AuditException {\n        return db.inTransaction(connection -> {\n            try (PreparedStatement read = connection.prepareStatement(sql);\n                ResultSet found = read.executeQuery()) {\n                found.next();\n                return Integer.valueOf(found.getInt(1));\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    public static final class AccountGuard {\n        private final int failures;\n        private final long windowStart;\n        private final long blockedUntil;\n\n        AccountGuard(int failures, long windowStart, long blockedUntil) {\n            this.failures = failures;\n            this.windowStart = windowStart;\n            this.blockedUntil = blockedUntil;\n        }\n\n        public int failures() {\n            return failures;\n        }\n\n        public long windowStart() {\n            return windowStart;\n        }\n\n        public boolean isBlocked(long nowEpochSeconds) {\n            return blockedUntil > nowEpochSeconds;\n        }\n\n        boolean windowExpired(long nowEpochSeconds) {\n            return (nowEpochSeconds - No externally supplied string -\n * email, username, password, token, file name - is ever passed to {@link #record}, so log\n * injection and secret leakage through the log are structural properties rather than\n * conventions someone has to remember. c : '_');\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Values are code-supplied, but they are still escaped and length-bounded so a future caller\n     * cannot break out of the JSON string or write a megabyte per event.\n     */\n    private static String escape(String value) {\n        String bounded = value == null ? \"\" : (value.length() > 256 ? value.substring(0, 256) : value);\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(bounded.length() + 8);\n        for (int i = 0; i < bounded.length(); i++) {\n            char c = bounded.charAt(i);\n            switch (c) {\n                case '\"' -> out.append(\"\\\\\\\"\");\n                case '\\\\' -> out.append(\"\\\\\\\\\");\n                case '\\n' -> out.append(\"\\\\n\");\n                case '\\r' -> out.append(\"\\\\r\");\n                case '\\t' -> out.append(\"\\\\t\");\n                default -> {\n                    if (c < 0x20 || c == 0x7f) {\n                        out.append(String.format(\"\\\\u%04x\", (int) c));\n                    } else {\n                        out.append(c);\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    /** Reads the trail, for tests and operator inspection. */\n    public List<String> readAll() throws IOException {\n        if (!Files.exists(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            return new ArrayList<>();\n        }\n        return Files.readAllLines(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n\n    public Path path() {\n        return file;\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public void close() {\n        // Records open the file per append so a long-lived handle cannot be redirected or held\n        // open by another process; there is nothing to close.\n    }\n\n    /** Audit failure. Without a\n * sweep the store only grows, and unbounded growth in a security table is its own problem: it\n * slows the very lookups that decide access and eventually exhausts the disk, which the audit\n * trail turns into a denial. So the state that can no longer affect a decision is deleted -\n * expired or revoked sessions, elapsed throttle windows, and guard rows for accounts that are both\n * outside the recent window <em>and</em> hold no live state.\n *\n * <p>A sweep never weakens a control that is still in force. A source bucket is eligible only once\n * its whole window has elapsed, which is the moment its next attempt would refill it anyway, so\n * deleting rows cannot be used to reset a denial that is currently blocking somebody. A guard row is\n * kept for the same reason even when its account has aged out of retention: if its lockout has not\n * expired, or its failure window is still running, deleting it would hand a rate limit or a lockout\n * back to whoever triggered the sweep.\n *\n * <p>Failure to sweep is reported and ignored, never fatal and never a denial: this is a\n * housekeeping task, and refusing a sign-in because old rows were not deleted would be a denial of\n * service caused by maintenance rather than by an attack.\n */\npublic final class Maintenance {\n\n    /** Guard rows are kept for this many of the most recently created accounts. */\n    static final int RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP = 10_000;\n\n    private final SessionStore sessions;\n    private final Throttle throttle;\n    private final Gate gate;\n\n    public Maintenance(SessionStore sessions, Throttle throttle, Gate gate) {\n        this.sessions = sessions;\n        this.throttle = throttle;\n        this.gate = gate;\n    }\n\n    /** Runs one sweep; returns the number of rows removed, which is zero when the sweep failed. */\n    public int run() {\n        int sessionsRemoved;\n        int throttleRemoved;\n        try {\n            sessionsRemoved = sessions.purgeExpired();\n            throttleRemoved = throttle.purgeStaleState(RECENT_ACCOUNTS_TO_KEEP);\n        } catch (SQLException | AuditLog.AuditException sweepFailed) {\n            System.err.println(\"NOTE the maintenance sweep could not complete;\"\n                + \" the operation that triggered it is unaffected\");\n            return 0;\n        }\n        int removed = sessionsRemoved + throttleRemoved;\n        // Only a sweep that changed state is recorded. This runs on every invocation, so a record per\n        // no-op would make the trail grow in proportion to how often the program is started - the one\n        // resource the audit budget exists to bound.\n        if (removed == 0) {\n            return 0;\n        }\n        try {\n            gate.record(AuditLog.Event.MAINTENANCE_RUN,\n                \"sessions\", Integer.toString(sessionsRemoved),\n                \"throttle\", Integer.toString(throttleRemoved));\n        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException auditUnwritable) {\n            // Nothing was denied by this record, so an unwritable trail must not undo a sweep that\n            // already committed.   String inline = env.apply(ENV_DB_PASSWORD);\n        String file = env.apply(ENV_DB_PASSWORD_FILE);\n        if (inline != null && !inline.isEmpty() && file != null && !file.isEmpty()) {\n static int indexOf(byte[] bytes, int length, byte target) {\n        for (int i = 0; i < length; i++) {\n            if (bytes[i] == target) {\n                return i;\n            }\n        }\n        return -1;\n    }\n\n    private static int boundedInt(java.util.function.Function<String, String> env, String name,\n        int fallback, int min, int max) throws ConfigException {\n        String value = env.apply(name);\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            return fallback;\n        }\n        int parsed;\n        try {\n            parsed = Integer.parseInt(value.trim());\n        } catch (NumberFormatException notANumber) {\n            throw new ConfigException(name + \" must be an integer between \" + min + \" and \" + max);\n        }\n        if (parsed < min || parsed > max) {\n            throw new ConfigException(name + \" must be between \" + min + \" and \" + max\n                + \"; the minimum keeps the password hashing String firstOption(String[] argv, String flag) throws ConfigException {\n        String prefix = flag + \"=\";\n        String found = null;\n        for (String argument : argv) {\n            if (argument.equals(flag) || argument.startsWith(prefix)) {\n                if (found != null) {\n                    throw new ConfigException(flag + \" may be given only once\");\n                }\n (Files.isSymbolicLink(dataDir)) {\n                throw new ConfigException(\"the data directory may not be a symbolic link\");\n            }\n            if (!Files.isDirectory(dataDir, ConfigException(\"the account store must have owner-only permissions (0600), found \"\n                    + PosixFilePermissions.toString(actual));\n            }\n            return;\n        }\n cannotCreate) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the account store could not be created\");\n        }\n        if (!OWNER_ONLY.equals(permissions(store))) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the account store could not be created with owner-only permissions\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Bounds the audit log so a flood cannot fill the filesystem; truncation is logged. */\n    static void ensureAuditLogWithinBudget(Path auditLog) throws ConfigException {\n        if (!Files.exists(auditLog, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            return;\n        }\n        if (Files.isSymbolicLink(auditLog)) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the audit log may not be a symbolic link\");\n        }\n        if (!Files.isRegularFile(auditLog, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the audit log must be a regular file\");\n        }\n        if (!OWNER_ONLY.equals(permissions(auditLog))) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the audit log must have owner-only permissions (0600)\");\n        }\n        // The size bound lives in AuditLog, so the startup bound and the append bound are one\n        // number, not two that can drift - and the retirement writes its marker inside the trail,\n        // not only on the terminal, so an erased history stays detectable from the file alone.\n        try {\n            new AuditLog(auditLog).retireOversizedTrail();\n        } catch (AuditLog.AuditException couldNotInspect) {\n            throw new ConfigException(\"the audit log could not be inspected\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static void rejectSymlinkComponents(Path path) throws ConfigException {\n        Path absolute = path.toAbsolutePath().normalize();\n        for (Path component = absolute; component != null && component.getNameCount() > 0;\n             component = component.getParent()) {\n            if (Files.isSymbolicLink(component)) {\n                throw         throw new ConfigException(\"permissions of \" + path.getFileName() + \" could not be determined\");\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isWorldWritable(Path path) throws ConfigException {\n        return permissions(path).contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE);\n    }\n\n    private static boolean hasGroupOrOtherAccess(Path path) throws ConfigException {\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> actual = permissions(path);\n        return actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n            || actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n            || actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_EXECUTE)\n            || actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n            || actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n            || actual.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_EXECUTE);\n    }\n\n    /** Marker so callers can distinguish a bad environment from a runtime failure. */\n    public static final class ConfigException serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n        AppConfiguredException(String safeDescription, Throwable cause) {\n            super(safeDescription, cause);\n        }\n
